# Unicorn SAL session — 2026-10-28, runs 73–80

Parent session (runs 67–72) is summarized in the main agent's context.
This note covers runs 73–80 only. Original `sal_emu.py` untouched throughout;
each run is a numbered copy (`sal_emuNN.py` + `runNN.txt` + `*NN` dumps).
Corpus files NEVER modified; staged copies live only under
`unicorn/scratch/root/Windows/system32/`.

## Run 73 — `_stat` (sal_emu73.py)
- Implemented MSVC `_stat` (36-byte `_stat32` layout; mode 0x8000|0x1FF,
  size/mtime/atime/ctime from the host file; missing → −1, errno 2).
  Also mirrored errno into the `_errno` cell from `_access`.
- Result: HLE 758. Engine stat'ed `C:\Windows\system32/omglog.txt`
  (missing → −1), then stopped on UNIMPLEMENTED `fopen`.

## Run 74 — CRT FILE* layer (sal_emu74.py)
- Implemented `fopen`, `_wfopen`, `fclose`, `fread`, `fwrite`, `fseek`,
  `ftell`, `fgets`, `fputs`, `vfprintf`, `fprintf`, `fflush`, `rewind`
  (Python files behind guest FILE* pointers; write/append modes create
  missing parent dirs, matching a real system32 that exists).
- Result: HLE 760. Engine opened `omglog.txt` in append mode, then
  stopped on UNIMPLEMENTED `localtime`.

## Run 75 — CRT time family (sal_emu75.py)
- Implemented `localtime`, `gmtime`, `mktime`, `asctime`
  (struct tm = 9 ints, 36 bytes; persistent buffers).
- Result: HLE 770. Engine logged to `omglog.txt` and then THREW:
  `OMGException: (0x12)<13126> - init.ocm: not found`
  (log line verbatim from the engine's own log file).
  Final stop: `_CxxThrowException` → harness abort by design.
- Lesson: the manager bootstrap requires `init.ocm` at
  `C:\Windows\system32\init.ocm` and throws when it is absent.
  The engine never tried the OpenMG dir path before throwing.

## Run 77 — FlushInstructionCache + full trace dump (sal_emu77.py)
- `FlushInstructionCache` → return 1 (TRUE; x86 icache is coherent,
  and Unicorn/QEMU invalidates translated blocks on guest writes).
  Full HLE trace dumped to `trace77.txt` (the log prints only
  first 120 + last 20 calls).
- Result: HLE 2633, SEH rounds 73. Fetch path fully traced:
  `CreateFileW` → `GetFileSize` (582,636) →
  `ReadFile(handle, 0x10358940, 582636)`.
  New stop: UNHANDLED single-step, code 0x80000004 at 0x6516aae6,
  chain head = 0x0 (no handler).
- Root cause of that stop (disassembled from image-data77.bin):
  the probe at 0x6516aa62 reads `fs:[0]` and overwrites `[head+4]`
  with ctxpush (0x65125420), assuming a node exists; with head = 0
  that write lands at linear 4. The 72 earlier single-steps in this
  run all dispatched fine (28 ctxpush + 44 splice, all disposition 0).

## Run 80 — `_set_se_translator` (sal_emu80.py)
- Registered `_set_se_translator` (all three key spellings: full
  mangled `?_set_se_translator@@YAP6AXIPAU_EXCEPTION_POINTERS@@@ZP6AXI0@Z@Z`,
  `msvcr71.dll!`-prefixed, and bare) as stdcall/1-arg returning 0
  (NULL = no previous translator; the engine passed 0x63917810,
  an OMGUtils address, as its translator).
- Result: HLE 3272, SEH rounds 111. Past the old death point, but
  dies on a NEW unhandled AV:
  `fault eip=0x651483b5 esp=0xf3fed7c eflags=0x00000086
  sr=invalid access type=19 addr=0xfffffffc size=4 pc=0x651483b5`
  Final dispatch: head node 0xf3fed7c (cached handler 0x65137909)
  returned 1 (continue); its next pointer is 0x40 (a repurposed
  node slot — the engine reuses node slots for VM context records
  between faults); node 0x40 is all zeros → handler 0x0 →
  "chain exhausted: bogus handler" → UNHANDLED.
- Deeper cause: the continuation that resumed at 0x65148379
  (ctxpush, Dr2=0x1aeb7c86 → Eip 0x65148379, EBP = node address,
  ESP = 0x8) runs code that reads a pointer from `[ebp-8]` and
  dereferences it; `[ebp-8]` held garbage (0xc896451b on one
  resume, 0/0xfffffff8 on the next). The frame below the node was
  never a valid frame for this continuation.
- CAUTION: salwrap.dll's FILE bytes at 0x65148379 decode as
  garbage, but the LIVE bytes at resume time were valid
  (`03 65 fc 8b 4d e8 8b 91` = add/mov/mov) — the engine patches
  its own .text at runtime (VirtualProtect + code writes +
  FlushInstructionCache throughout). Static .text disassembly
  LIES for these regions; only live-memory dumps count.

## Ruled out along the way
- FS-base mismatch: engine's SetThreadContext calls use ONLY flags
  0x10010 (CONTEXT_DEBUG_REGISTERS, 290 calls in run 77) — it never
  asks to change segments, so fs:[0] = linear 0 is correct.
- Vectored handlers: no AddVectoredExceptionHandler calls anywhere.
- `__CxxFrameHandler` in HLE is `h_ret1` (always CONTINUE_SEARCH);
  a C++ catch that should handle an exception cannot be honored by
  the harness today — relevant if a future fault needs a real catch.

## Next investigation (not a one-stub fix)
1. Dump LIVE .text (not .data) at run end so 0x65148379's real code
   can be disassembled.
2. Determine what `[ebp-8]` should hold for the 0x65148379
   continuation: trace the stack history below node 0xf3fed7c —
   what the ctxpush handler's edited ctx (EBP = node, ESP = 0x8)
   expects vs what the harness's stack actually contains.
3. Only then decide whether the fix is in the continuation
   machinery (ctxpush/splice context editing), the chain-walk
   (repurposed next pointers), or a missing harness feature
   (e.g., real C++ catch handling via __CxxFrameHandler).

## Continuation — runs 82–86 (2026-10-29, ~02:00–02:30 PDT)

Starting state verified from files: run 80 stall reproduced structurally in
runs 81–84 (SEH rounds 111, UNHANDLED, manager ctx 0x1 leftover). The
continuation's brief ("[ebp-8] garbage") was refined by live dumps into a
full causal chain; two real fixes landed; criterion (a) is now MET.

### Run 81 (instrumentation only, reported in prior summary)
Reproduced run 80's stall at a different stub address (0x6514e5a9, +0x6230
from run 80's 0x65148379) — VM-generated stub code in salwrap .data
relocates per run. Fault = MOV EAX,[EDX+4] with EDX = cursor-8, NOT
[EBP-4]/[EBX-4] (run 81 fault addr 0xf83b1e00 = EDX+4 with EBX=0 rules
out the EBX theory).

### Run 82 (instrumentation only: live dump on EVERY resume + frame slots)
- Reproduced again: stub 0x6514fca9, SEH 111, UNHANDLED, ctx 0x1.
- Stub fully decoded from live .data (identical bytes all runs, only the
  base moves): a VM-stack pop routine. Frame protocol: [EBP-0x18] = VM
  context object pointer; +0x1B4 = stack cursor (decremented by 8, checked
  against ctx+0x24 = base; JE to a normal epilogue+RET when empty).
  Fault path: cursor 0 -> cursor-8 wraps to 0xFFFFFFF8 -> check vs base
  fails -> MOV EAX,[EDX+4] faults at 0xFFFFFFFC.
- Real VM frames share [EBP-0x18] = heap ctx object (run 82: 0x107e6ba4)
  and constants [EBP-0x8]=2304, [EBP-0x4]=2080. At end of run 82 the ctx
  object's cursor (+0x1B4) == base (ctx+0x24) -> VM stack EMPTY, and the
  ctx object holds a routine table, Dr2 selectors, and path strings
  ("C:\Windows\system32/procfile/", ".../procfile/Device.sal").
- ctxpush decoded (static .text, trustworthy): pop-one-frame protocol
  (new EBP=[old EBP], new ESP=old EBP+8, new EIP=Dr2^0x7FFFFFFF,
  EDI/ESI/EBX from fault ESP). Its "unlink" does NOT pop the head:
  head->next = head->next->next (deletes the SECOND node); fs:[0] itself
  is unchanged. This resolves the run-80 paradox (edited EBP = pre-unlink
  head): with EBP=0, ctxpush reads [0] = head pointer (TEB-at-0 harness
  artifact; on real Windows [0] would fault) and resumes the stub with
  EBP = a node address -> garbage ctx -> cursor fault -> the stub's own
  pushes (PUSH 0x40) overwrite head->next with 0x40 -> walk hits
  all-zeros node 0x40 -> chain exhausted -> UNHANDLED. Chain exhaustion
  is a SYMPTOM of the bogus frame, not the root cause.
- Candidate verdicts at this point: (a) ctxpush editing CORRECT, not the
  bug; (b) chain-walk next-pointer caching REJECTED (would loop: same
  stub resume, same fault, forever); (c) C++ catch REJECTED for stub
  faults (generated .data code has no xdata).

### Run 83 (instrumentation only: EIP ring buffer, armed at round 95)
- Reproduced again: SEH 111, UNHANDLED. Ring captured the pivotal fault:
  INVALID INSTRUCTION (0xC000001D) at stack EIP 0xf3fee93, identical
  target in runs 82 and 83 (deterministic path).
- Ring tail before the fault: ... salwrap caller 0x65116360 (SEH-prologue
  function, handler 0x65137909; file .text matches ring boundaries
  exactly) -> call [0x65139290] -> OMGUtils 0x639178e0 -> hook page
  0x20000e50 -> fallthrough -> RET at 0x639178f6 -> stack 0xf3fee90
  -> 0xf3fee92 -> fault at 0xf3fee93. So the wild transfer is a RET
  from OMGUtils code to a stack address, NOT a stub fault.

### Run 84 (instrumentation only: live OMGUtils .text dump on 0xC000001D)
- Reproduced again: SEH 111, UNHANDLED. Live dump decoded the wrapper
  exactly (matches ring EIPs e0,e1,e6,e8,ee,f0,f3,f5,f6):
    0x639178e0: push esi
    0x639178e1: push 0x63917810      ; SE translator fn address
    0x639178e6: mov esi, ecx
    0x639178e8: call [0x6392236c]    ; MSVCR71 IAT slot #53
    0x639178ee: mov [esi], eax
    0x639178f0: add esp, 4           ; wrapper cleans its own arg
    0x639178f3: mov eax, esi
    0x639178f5: pop esi
    0x639178f6: ret
- IAT slot [0x6392236c] = MSVCR71 import #53 = _set_se_translator
  (NOT strtol; an earlier stub-index reading was off by one because the
  load-phase enumeration script skipped the halt stub at idx 0).
  The LocalFree slot [0x639221a4] was verified INTACT at fault time
  (0x20000450 = LocalFree stub idx 69) — no engine redirection there.
- ROOT CAUSE (run 84): the wrapper cleans its own pushed arg
  (`add esp, 4`), i.e. it was written for a CDECL callee. The harness
  registered _set_se_translator as STDCALL (all three keys: mangled,
  msvcr71.dll!-prefixed, bare). The stub therefore ALSO cleaned 4
  bytes -> DOUBLE CLEANUP -> ESP 4 too high -> `pop esi` ate the
  wrapper's return address -> `ret` popped caller stack data
  (0xf3fee90) as the return address -> wild jump -> illegal
  instruction -> handler walk -> ctxpush pops -> stub rounds on
  bogus frames -> chain exhaustion -> UNHANDLED. The mangled name
  itself proves cdecl: `?_set_se_translator@@YAP6AX...` (@@YA = cdecl).

### Run 94 — loop-entry instrumentation (diagnostic only, no behavior change)
- Added a one-shot UC_HOOK_CODE log at the SID loop fn entry (OMGUtils
  0x63919e40): ret addr, array pointer, arg2 (wanted SID), arg3, count,
  entries. Result: ret=0x6391a235 (call inside the classifier fn starting
  ~0x6391a0d0), array=0x107e93d0, arg2=0x107e93f0 (S-1-5-32-544
  Administrators), arg3=0x107e6db0, and [array]=0x107E93E0 — a POINTER, not
  a count. Heap dump read at 0x107e93d0 confirmed the layout: +0x0 pointer
  0x107e93e0, +0x4 NULL (entry0 SID), +0x8/+0xc uninitialized garbage
  (0x26558c51, 0x26d58bcf), +0x10 the stub's fabricated token SID (rev 1,
  1 subauth, auth 5, RID 1000), +0x20 the Administrators SID.
- Caller disassembly (0x6391a1c0–0x6391a235) proved the root cause: the
  caller does the standard two-call GetTokenInformation pattern with
  TokenInformationClass=2 (TokenGroups), allocates the returned length,
  then calls the loop fn with (groups buffer, Administrators SID).
  The harness's h_GetTokenInformation ignored the class and always wrote
  TOKEN_USER-shaped data (SID pointer + 0), so the caller read
  GroupCount = a SID pointer (huge) and walked uninitialized heap as
  group entries: entry0 SID NULL (EqualSid false, continue), entry1 SID
  0x26d58bcf garbage -> fault. DIAGNOSIS CONFIRMED, no HLE gap in
  EqualSid itself.

### b650 identified (Ghidra, out/decomp-key.c + out/phase3-decomp.c)
- SAL_BASE+0x1b650 = FUN_6511b650, __thiscall "salexec": (this=ctx,
  spec struct, out, name struct). The struct layout matches the probe's
  _mkstr exactly: {dword; std::string at +4} with SSO (capacity at
  +0x18; heap pointer at +4 iff capacity > 0xf; size at +0x14).
  Flow: virtual lock call on this+0x80, trace the name via
  FUN_65125350, optional QueryPerformanceCounter timing into the
  this+0x4b0 stats table, parse via FUN_65116360 (thin SE-translator
  wrapper) -> FUN_651349f0 -> FUN_6512c840 (parser/compiler object
  DAT_6516d484), then execute via FUN_65106db0(out, parsed, status).
- CONCLUSION: b650 is the SAL script executor, not a bare "encrypt"
  entry point. The probe's spec='Encrypt' is fed to the SAL
  parser/compiler as script text (or a script to resolve), and the
  engine's service dispatch/validation rejects it with "poi poi poi".
  The next step is NOT another HLE fix: it is designing a VALID salexec
  spec — i.e. learning the SAL script format (device.sal's solved
  SalAsn grammar) for invoking an encrypt operation, or finding the
  real encrypt entry point elsewhere. No further harness change is
  indicated until the spec is valid.
- OPEN (not blocking yet): the EKB directory probe exists=False was
  tolerated; whether a real EKB dir matters for actual encryption is
  unknown. The earlier icv.dat probe (runs 92/93) is a different file
  from icv.ocm and remains unstaged/unprobed further.

## Session 5 — runs 99–106 (2026-10-29): the "poi poi poi" gate falls; first clean salexec

### Desk reframing (before run 99) — session 4's conclusion was wrong
- FUN_6512c840 is NOT a text parser/compiler: it is the VM INVOKER. It
  builds a parameter block {next, executor obj, spec dword0, spec text
  ptr, spec len}, points Dr1 at it (sign-flip encoding) and calls the
  generated stub through Dr3 ^ 0x7fffffff with (FUN_6512cbf0, obj).
  FUN_6512cbf0 relocates the 0x800-byte stub page to an rdtsc-randomized
  block-pool address, re-points Dr1 at a second block, and calls the
  stub. The spec (dword0, text, len) is DATA for the stub.
- The "poi poi poi" throw site is FUN_65107d70 (417 B, ret 8). It is a
  file-group VERIFY: this+0x14 = 'C:\Windows\system32\icv.dat' (later
  runs: the Repository-rooted path), records at this+0x2c =
  maclist.dat / maclist1.dat / maclist2.dat. It GetFileAttributesA's
  the three, writeToFile's around the first missing one, calls a
  service via omgutil9's object (vtable+0x44), then
  FUN_65103e50(this) -> FUN_651161b0(subobj); 0 -> throw (0x307).
  FUN_65107d70's only callers are the five module handlers
  FUN_6510b490 (maclist), FUN_6510b880, FUN_6510c260, FUN_6510c690,
  FUN_6510ca30 (icv/maclist service handler).
- FUN_651161b0 dispatches on OMGUtils `omgutil` (export 278): omgutil(n)
  compares the OS-class fn 0x63919d40 against n-thresholds. In this
  harness it took the FUN_65115b20 (verifyB) branch, not verifyA.

### Run 103 — the wild jump is a HARNESS BUG (diagnostic)
- [s2] hooks: FUN_65114810's struct fields are 'SOFTWARE\Sony

- VERDICT on doors: salexec (b650 via vt[0]) IS the right door — it

## Session 7 — run 108+ (2026-10-29): the atpcd.ocm verify wall, characterized

### Desk findings BEFORE run 108 (Ghidra papers + Python, engine-exact)
- **FUN_65119f20 is a device.sal-ONLY registrar.** Its decompilation
  (out/phase3-decomp.c) hardcodes: FUN_65101000(param_1, param_2, **1**,
  &ver, &algo) — the verify-mode argument is the constant 1, identical
  for every caller input — and on success it writes the image out via
  omgResolveRepositoryFile("device.sal") and "procfile/device.sal",
  logging "restart required for new device.sal: (ver:%d, algo:%d)".
  The throw string "device.sal verify failed" is literal: this
  function exists to (re)register device.sal and nothing else. There
  is NO call variant of it that accepts an .ocm envelope.
- FUN_65101000 semantics (out/decomp-loader.c), exact: if LE u32@+2
  == 0x01000000 AND LE u32@+6 == 0x01000000 -> outs = 1,1, return 1
  (no CRC). Else *out1 = BE16@+4, *out2 = BE16@+2; if mode != 0, run
  CRC-32 (std poly, init 0xFFFFFFFF) over the whole buffer where
  bytes at 0-based offsets 6..9 contribute table index (crc & 0xff)
  — i.e. the stored field's own value is replaced by a function of
  the running CRC (self-referential) — and return 0 iff ~crc != LE
  u32@+6. If mode == 0 the CRC is skipped and it ALWAYS returns 1.
- **Engine-exact CRC replication** (table-driven, index rule above;
  NOTE: binascii.crc32 with zero-fed bytes 6..9 does NOT reproduce
  the engine — the hand-rolled loop below does, verified on
  device.sal): device.sal computed 0x4bbbc796 == stored 0x4bbbc796
  PASS. atpcd.ocm computed 0x49dc003c vs stored 0x00002c10 FAIL
  (agrees with run 107's in-engine result).
- **Envelope comparison (corpus, first 16 bytes):**
  device.sal: 03 01 00 01 | 19 44 | 96 c7 bb 4b | 01 01 04 08 6a 35
    -> BE16@+2 = 0x0001 (algo), BE16@+4 = 0x1944 (ver), +6 = real CRC.
  atpcd.ocm:  03 02 00 00 | 05 00 | 10 2c 00 00 | 4d 47 ca 99 6a b2
    -> BE16@+2 = 0x0000, BE16@+4 = 0x0500, +6 word = 0x00002c10.
  EVERY corpus .ocm (class byte1 0x01 and 0x02 alike: init2, ekbman,
  icv, maclist, region, mp4comp, Omgtomp3, ...) has bytes 2..5 =
  00 00 05 00 and the SAME constant 0x00002c10 at +6 (older-rev
  modules bot-dnk/hdd-dnk/importer_key have 01 00 at +4 and
  0x00000000 at +6). The +6 word in the .ocm envelope is a FORMAT
  CONSTANT, not a CRC — no content CRC can match a constant across
  files. init.ocm is a 7-byte wrapper (04 83 08 47 50 ...) around an
  inner module whose header matches the icv/Omgtomp3 family.
- Hypothesis verdicts (desk): (a) class-arg — REFUTED as a route:
  mode is hardcoded 1 inside the device.sal registrar; and class
  byte1 0x01 .ocm files fail too, so it is the whole .ocm envelope
  family that is foreign to FUN_65101000, not the 0x02 class.
  (b) different CRC feed — REFUTED: engine-exact feed pinned by
  device.sal's pass; atpcd's +6 is a constant shared by all .ocm.
  (c) byte-swap/offset — REFUTED: offsets proven by device.sal.
  (d) wrong length — REFUTED: atpcd.ocm is 21,564 B on disk
  (0x543C, MD5 e4ae53cc186dbde18dae8e57bd73dcb7 == staged copy),
  exactly what run 107 passed.
- CONCLUSION: the wall is a category error, not a field subtlety.
  atpcd.ocm can NEVER pass FUN_65119f20 — by design. .ocm modules
  load through a different path (the one D680's bootstrap used for
  init.ocm). Run 108 confirms the wall in-engine and probes
  FUN_65101000 directly (mode 1 vs mode 0) as oracle for the above.

### Run 108 — wall reproduced + FUN_65101000 probed directly (oracle)
Base: sal_emu108.py (copy of sal_emu107.py, probes replaced).
Bootstrap clean: D680 final stop None, fresh ctx = 0x100002e0
(DAT_6516b07c agrees). b650 reproduce105: SEH 151, EAX=0, out
{01 00} — run-105 recipe intact. Getter baseline match=True
(0x1944, 0x1, 0x11944); getimage baseline == device.sal byte-exact.
Direct __cdecl calls to FUN_65101000(buf, len, mode, &o1, &o2):
- device.sal, mode 1 -> EAX=1, out1 = 0x1944, out2 = 0x1.
- atpcd.ocm, mode 1 -> EAX=0, out1 = 0x0500, out2 = 0x0000.
- atpcd.ocm, mode 0 -> EAX=1, out1 = 0x0500, out2 = 0x0000
  (CRC skipped, exactly as the decompilation reads).
- device.sal, mode 0 -> EAX=1, out1 = 0x1944, out2 = 0x1.
FUN_65119f20 control: device.sal -> EAX=1, ctx[0,4,8,3c] unchanged
(version gate), getter/getimage after unchanged. atpcd.ocm ->
watch hook shows FUN_65101000 entered with a3 = 0x1 (mode hardcoded
1, live-confirmed), EAX=0; engine built "(0xe) <60268> - device.sal
verify failed" and threw via _CxxThrowException; probe aborted
there, ctx unchanged. THE WALL STANDS EXACTLY AS RECORDED IN RUN
107 — and is now fully explained: it is the device.sal registrar
rejecting a foreign envelope family, not a corrupt file.

### Verdicts after run 108 — the wall is SOLVED as a characterization
- Goal 1 (reproduce control): MET. Bootstrap clean, ctx fresh,
  device.sal passes FUN_65101000 both directly (mode 1) and via
  FUN_65119f20; atpcd.ocm fails both, throw reproduced verbatim.
- Goal 2 (hypotheses): ALL FOUR resolved — (a) no class-arg route
  exists (mode hardcoded 1; class-0x01 .ocm files fail too);
  (b) CRC feed pinned exactly (device.sal passes; atpcd's +6 word
  is a cross-file constant 0x00002c10, not a CRC); (c) offsets
  proven; (d) length correct (21,564 B = 0x543C on disk and staged,
  MD5 e4ae53cc186dbde18dae8e57bd73dcb7 both).
- Goal 3 (register -> entry table -> encrypt): NOT REACHABLE via
  FUN_65119f20, by design — it is the device.sal registrar only
  (hardcoded "device.sal" filename on the success path). No
  registration of atpcd.ocm is possible through it, so no entry
  table, no encrypt attempt, no pairs/ comparison this session.
  NO cipher claim.
- Goal 4 (precise named sub-problem): THE .ocm LOADER IS
  FUN_65101730(out_struct, name) — called by D680 itself at
  bootstrap with "init.ocm" and again with "region.ocm"
  (out/phase3-decomp.c D680 body). FUN_65101730's own decompilation
  is NOT in the current Ghidra papers (only call sites). NEXT
  SESSION: decompile/trace FUN_65101730, follow it to the class-02
  envelope parser (the 03 01/03 02 header family with the 0x2c10
  constant), and drive that path for "atpcd.ocm" the way bootstrap
  drives it for region.ocm. That — not the .sal registrar — is the
  door to atpcd's entry table and the burn-time encrypt routine.
- Harness changes live only in sal_emu108.py; sal_emu.py original
  untouched; Sony corpus untouched (all reads; staged copies

## Session 8 — run 109+ (2026-10-29): FUN_65101730, the real .ocm loader

### Desk findings BEFORE run 109 (papers re-read; D680 body in phase3-decomp.c)
- Starting state re-verified against Sessions 5–7 above: bootstrap
  clean, ctx re-derived per run (0x100002e0), ctx PROVEN (run 107),
  FUN_65119f20 closed as the device.sal-only registrar (run 108).
- D680's init body (phase3-decomp.c) shows the loader's call shape:
  FUN_65101730 takes (ret_storage, name_string) and RETURNS a
  pointer used as a std::string* — i.e. it returns a std::string BY
  VALUE (hidden first arg), name in arg 2. Three bootstrap calls:
  (1) "init.ocm" — return value DISCARDED in D680; (2) "region.ocm"
  -> FUN_65101a80(auStack_98, ret) ; (3) "region.ocm" again ->
  FUN_65101a80(auStack_cc, ret). FUN_651017c0 / FUN_65101780 are
  called on the local string/struct objects afterwards
  (cleanup-shaped). So 01730 itself is plausibly the file FETCH
  (read repository file into a string — consistent with runs 76/86
  observing verbatim fetches) and FUN_65101a80 is the consumer that
  does something with the bytes (parse/register into a local
  object). TO BE CONFIRMED by decompiling both.
- FUN_65101730 / 65101780 / 651017c0 / 65101a80 decompilations are
  NOT in out/ (fns/ covers only 0x65125xxx–0x65134xxx). Ghidra
  project proj/SalWrap + JDK21 + Ghidra 12.1.4 present; new script
  scripts/SalDecompile01730.java written in the SalDecompile2.java

### Decompile result — the brief's premise about 01730 is REFUTED
- **FUN_65101730 (68 B) is the MSVC std::string COPY CONSTRUCTOR**:
  body = SEH frame + basic_string::basic_string(this, src) + return
  this. It touches no file, registry, or parser. Its neighbours are
  the same STL family: FUN_65101780 (54 B) = string destructor;
  FUN_651017c0 (63 B) = destructor of a struct's +8 string member;
  FUN_65101a80 (90 B) = constructor of a record {0, 0, string-copy}.
  Full text in out/decomp-loader01730.c (new paper).
- Session 7's "FUN_65101730(out_struct, name) is the .ocm loader"
  was a call-site misreading: in FUN_6511c8a0 (D680's 1,947 B init
  body; D680 itself is 213 B: alloc 0x4d0, ctor FUN_6511d5b0, then
  c8a0) the 01730 calls COPY the "init.ocm"/"region.ocm" name
  strings into locals; the actual fetch/parse happens elsewhere in
  c8a0 (indirect calls via the PTR_PTR_651411dc table and/or the VM
  invoker FUN_6512c840 via thunk FUN_65134a10). Same failure mode
  as session 6's "GetImplementationCount" garble: a plausible name
  pinned on an un-decompiled address. The session goal stands (find
  and drive the real bootstrap load path); the named door does not.
- Run 109 design (sal_emu109.py; harness fetch hooks added in
  h_CreateFileA/h_ReadFile behind a per-run callback dict): code
  hooks on c8a0 entry (capture obj ptr), 01730, FUN_6512c840,
  FUN_65134a10, FUN_6512c7e0/c810; CreateFile/ReadFile hooks logging
  every .ocm open with EBP-chain + stack-scan return addresses and
  0x4d0-byte snapshots of the manager object at each fetch (diffed
  post-D680); PTR_651411dc table dump post-bootstrap; controls =
  run-105 b650 recipe + getter + getimage baselines.

### Run 109 — bootstrap load path traced end-to-end (sal_emu109.py)

  +0x3c = FUN_6511c550 wraps it. NO parsing in the fetcher.
- **Execution IS the load**: c8a0 takes the fetcher's (ptr, len) and
  calls thunk FUN_65134a10(ptr, len, &outstr) ->
  FUN_6512c840(vmobj = *DAT_6516d488, ptr, len, outstr) — the file
  bytes are executed as a SAL program in the INIT VM. Trace proof:
  ReadFile init.ocm -> buf 0x10358b00 n=582636; next event thunk-
  34a10 a1=0x10358b00 a2=0x8e3ec (=582636) a3=out; c840 vmctx=
  0x10330868 == *DAT_6516d488.
- Two VM ctx objects: *DAT_6516d488 = init VM (arena setup
  FUN_6512c7e0, blob DAT_65158050, "InitOcmMemorySize2");
  *DAT_6516d484 = main VM (arena FUN_6512c810, blob DAT_65145010,

- Manager-object snapdiffs: c8a0-entry -> fetch:init.ocm = NO

  +0x4bc..+0x4bf) — i.e. ALL manager registration state lands
  during the init.ocm VM execution window (inside which the VM
  itself probed device.sal + procfile/device.sal via _access);
  fetch:region.ocm -> post-D680 = 1 B at +0x7c (a flag).
- PTR_651411dc table == 0x65139fb8 = the MANAGER VTABLE (slot

  FUN_6511a540, +0x48 = FUN_6511a560 — all in the new paper).

### Desk findings BEFORE run 112 (grammar)
- ALL THREE captured records parse EXACTLY as a sequence of typed
  stream values + terminator, under this tag table (encoder-side
  conventions from sal_stream_tests.py readout: FUN_65124cc0 =
  INTEGER encoder, FUN_65125020 = OCTET STRING encoder):
  0x01 = int, 1-byte payload; 0x02 = int, 2-byte payload;
  0x03 = int, DER-length payload; 0x04 = octet string, DER-length,
  inline payload; 0x05 = buffer REFERENCE {u32 LE len, u32 LE ptr}
  to out-of-stream memory; 0x0b = terminator (no payload).
  Consumption: R1 = 9+2+1 = 12 B; R2 = 9+2+9+2+1 = 23 B;
  R3 = 6+6+2+9+2+1 = 26 B — all exact.
  R1 = [ref(device.sal image,15085), i1(0), END]
  R2 = [ref(60, 0x65144fd0), i1(2), ref(region image,11663),
        i1(0), END]
  R3 = [oct(4)=10 00 08 60, oct(4)=65 15 80 10, i1(3),
        ref(region image,11663), i1(0), END]
- FUN_6512da00 (native value converter, engine-decomp.c ~13930)
  CONFIRMS tag 1/2 integer encoding: signed, payload BIG-ENDIAN
  (fits int8 -> tag 1 + 1 B; else tag 2 + [hi, lo]). Its source
  value descriptor carries the kind in bits >>0x1e; kind 3 ->
  raises exactly 0x14402010 (ERR_TYPE). The 0x05 pair, by contrast,
  is LITTLE-endian in the record bytes (run 111 found the pair by
  LE pack search) — a native {len, ptr} struct embedded raw.
- 0x65144fd0 cell: at rest in salwrap.dll the 60 bytes are ALL ZERO
  (.bss); decomp-c8a0-helpers.c line 298 shows c8a0-era code ASSIGNS
  _DAT_65144fd0 = CoTaskMemAlloc_exref. So field1 of R2 references a
  60-byte LIVE table whose first cell is written at runtime (host
  fn-pointer table is the standing reading — to be dumped live in
  run 112, not assumed).
- Ignite pairs VERIFIED from files this session: TRK000A0-A3.ATP in
  atrac-cd-stage0/CD stuff/HIFI/, each 1,296,064 B; payload (after
  the 1,120 B prefix) = 1,294,944 B == len(ignite.raw) exactly.
  Secure header at file+1024: BlockSeed = 8 B at hdr+0x18 (file
  1048..1055, differs per file), InitVector = 8 B at hdr+0x58 (file
  1112..1119, differs per file); hdr+0x50 holds 2a d1 43 87 dd fb
  4b 86 IDENTICAL in all four. A0 seed = d8 83 bf 8b 43 59 d7 c6,
  A0 IV = 25 a6 71 27 6b 52 9c 02 (bytes at hdr+0x58..0x5f).
  Header diffs outside seed/IV are confined to the ID3 region.
- Run 112 plan (sal_emu112.py): reproduce baseline controls +
  atpcd fetch + V0 = exact run-111 transplant (wrapped shape);
  then single-field variants V1..V9 (drop field1 / drop int /
  int 2->0,1,3 / field1 -> zeroed heap buf / field1 -> heap copy of
  live table / R3-shape transplant / module-ref first). Oracle =
  final stop (None vs _CxxThrowException abort), SEH rounds, exact
  error string in log, outstr, ctx diff, watched-buffer diffs.

  host-function table (in the real DLL its first cell is assigned

  PASS; ctx diff 0 and all watched buffers unchanged throughout):
  V0 control [tbl, i1(2), mod, i1(0)] -> THROW ERR_TYPE
    (kind 0x14402010, LYR_SAL/MT_COM) "in Encrypt", 13 rds.

  V2 [i1(2), mod, i1(0)] (tbl dropped) -> THROW ERR_TYPE

  V4 int 2->1 -> THROW ERR_TYPE 0x14402010 "in Encrypt", 13 rds.

  V7 field1 -> ref(60, heap copy of live table), int=2 -> THROW

    -> THROW ERR_TYPE_VECTOR 0x14402014 "in Encrypt", 13 rds.
    (matches V5: the int VALUE 3 drives the vector error, not the
    octet strings before it)
  V9 [mod, i1(2), tbl, i1(0)] (module ref FIRST) -> THROW
    ERR_VERSION (kind 0x14c02010, LYR_MGLM / MT_INTRINSICS)
    "in Encrypt", 13 rds. Field ORDER matters; module ref does
    not belong in position 0.
- WORKING MODEL (fits all 10 data points): the record is
  [args..., INDEX, module-ref, trailing i1(0) framing] — the int
  immediately before the module ref selects the module ENTRY;
  fields before it are that entry's arguments; dispatch consumes
  the module ref as target (never an argument). Evidence: index
  alone changes the outcome class (0 clean / 1,2 ERR_TYPE /
  3 ERR_TYPE_VECTOR); V1/V2 (no [arg,index] pair before the ref)
  still land "in Encrypt" and fail — consistent with the pair
  being required and the VM defaulting/misreading without it;
  V9 shows the ref's position is structural, not positional-free.
  atpcd entry map so far: entry 0 accepts [tblref] and RETURNS
  (result not yet inspected — EAX points into heap, outstr
  empty); entry 1 rejects [tblref] ERR_TYPE; entry 2 = "Encrypt"
  rejects [tblref] ERR_TYPE (its first param is not a host-table
  ref); entry 3 rejects [tblref] ERR_TYPE_VECTOR.
- Run 113 plan: (a) re-run V3/entry-0 and DUMP the result memory
  at EAX (parse as stream); (b) entry-2 (Encrypt) argument scan:
  arg0 candidates ref(plaintext), ref(seediv), oct(seed||iv),
  i1(0), none; then pairs (plain,seediv), (plain,outbuf) etc.,

  (the ref arg materialized inline as an octet string), followed
  by 0x10-strided VM-arena pointer entries (0x103f0xxx) — EAX
  points at a VM arena block holding the materialized input/
  result stream, NOT a plain return value. W0/W0c dumps start
  `04 5e <94 random-looking bytes with a repeated 32-byte motif>`
  then `02 01 00 02 01 00 00 00 04 08 <8 bytes>` — a longer,

- ** ORDER-DEPENDENCE WARNING: run 114's E-series contradicts
  run 112's V0 for the IDENTICAL record [tbl, i1(2), mod, i1(0)]
  (ERR_VERSION here vs ERR_TYPE in runs 111/112). The only
  difference: run 114 executed clean index-0 calls (W0...) BEFORE
  any index-2 call; runs 111/112 hit index 2 first. The VM is a
  stateful continuation machine — invoke outcomes depend on
  history. The run-112 "response map" and the entry-index model
  are therefore NOT state-free facts. E13 landing (trailing 1)
  may be a trailing-field effect, a state effect, or both.

- P1/P2 [tbl, i1(2), mod, i1(1)] -> CLEAN 21 rds, repeatable.
  P3 [plain, 2, mod, 1] -> CLEAN. P4 [seediv, 2, mod, 1] ->
  CLEAN. P5 [plain, seediv, 2, mod, 1] -> CLEAN.
  P6 [plain, seediv, out, 2, mod, 1] -> CLEAN.
  P7 [tbl, 2, mod, 0] after the clean calls -> THROW ERR_TYPE
  0x14402010 (NOT ERR_VERSION — run 114's VERSION series is not
  reproduced by clean SEL=2/TAIL=1 history; its trigger was the
  SEL=0 calls, or remains unexplained). P8 [oct(plain376), 2,
  mod, 1] -> CLEAN.
- RESULT-AREA READING — the memory at EAX is the ARGUMENT
  MATERIALIZATION area, proven byte-exact: its first value is
  an octet string holding the LAST pre-SEL argument, dereferenced
  and copied inline: P1/P2 -> OCT(60) = table bytes; P3 ->
  OCT(8192) == ignite.raw[:8192] VERBATIM (checked in Python);
  P4 -> OCT(16) == seed||IV verbatim; P5 -> OCT(16) = seediv
  (last arg); P6 -> OCT(8192) = 0xAA out-buffer content (last
  arg); P8 -> OCT(376) == ignite[:376]. NO transformation of any
  argument in any landed call; NO watched buffer EVER changed;
  ctx diff 0 throughout.
- OFFLINE HEAP SEARCH (heap-dump110/111/112/114/115/121): the
  A0 ciphertext (TRK000A0 payload) 64-byte windows at offsets
  0/376/752/4096 appear NOWHERE. Ignite plaintext windows appear
  only as my own buffers/materializations. No landed call has
  encrypted anything under A0 parameters.
- REVISED UNDERSTANDING: SEL=2/TAIL=1 calls are accepted and the
  record is consumed (args materialized), but the operation
  performs no data work — consistent with a multi-invoke
  CONVERSATION (continuation machine): the landed call may only
  OPEN/select the operation, with data expected in FOLLOW-UP
  invokes (bootstrap itself used R2-then-R3 sequences for one
  module). Run 122 tests bare continuation feeds after an
  opening [tbl,2,mod,1], with full 16 MB heap-window diffs and
  an A0-ciphertext search after every invoke. If feeds stay
  inert, the next named step is decompiling omgatpcd.dll's
  IOmgAtpcdMan methods (the real request producer; imports
  salwrap0/salwrap1) to capture the exact request it builds.

### Runs 122 + omgatpcd desk — the DRIVER's real protocol found
- Run 122 (sal_emu122.py): conversation test. C1 [tbl,2,atpcd,1]
  landed (21 rds). Bare continuation feeds C2 [plain], C3
  [seediv], C4 [plain,seediv], C5 [oct(plain376)], C7 [out] ALL
  returned cleanly (25 rds, EAX=0) with only 28-39 B heap
  bookkeeping diffs - accepted but INERT (plaintext not even
  materialized). C6 [i1(0)] threw ERR_VERSION 0x14c02010
  (MGLM/INTRINSICS). C8 R3-shape threw ERR_TYPE 0x14402010.
  No A0 ciphertext anywhere; no watched buffer ever changed.
- DESK (new paper out/decomp-omgatpcd.c, Ghidra proj-atpcd):
  omgatpcd.dll (base 0x61600000) imports salwrap0/salwrap1. Its
  methods build requests as TYPED STREAMS via manager vtable
  writer slots and execute via manager vt[0]:
  ctor +0x40 (returns stream handle), write-octet +0x6c
  (FUN_6511d090: tag 04 + DER len + bytes, desc {ptr,len}),
  write-buffer +0x70 (FUN_6511d040: tag 05 + {len,ptr} LE),
  write-int +0x74 (FUN_6511c570), write-module +0x64
  (FUN_6511c760: module by NAME if desc[0]==0 - writes name via
  +0x68, else buffer-ref via +0x70; then appends 01 00 0b =
  i1(0)+END - THE TRAILING BYTES OF EVERY CAPTURED RECORD),
  execute vt[0] (FUN_6511d760 -> b650(spec=STREAM OBJECT, out,
  name from streamobj+0x24 or param)). Readers: +0x8c byte,
  +0x84 {ptr,len}, +0x80 string, +0x9c int.
  Live vtable read from the DLL file @0x65139fb8 matches these
  slots (+0x00 d760, +0x64 c760, +0x6c d090, +0x70 d040,
  +0x74 c570). NOTE: session 7's slot labels for +0x40/+0x44/
  +0x64/+0x6c (cde0/9f20/af70/cbd0) were a DIFFERENT object's
  table - FUN_6511cde0 is in fact salwrap's own region-request
  BUILDER (it builds R2/R3 with exactly these writers).
  FUN_65116e40 builds {u32 BE} octet descriptors - R3's leading
  octets are BE-serialized addresses (0x10000860, 0x65158010).
- FUN_616054cd (omgatpcd, complete minimal op "OP-A"):
  request = [octet16(manager vt+8's 16-byte value), int(0),
  module("atpcd.ocm")] via writers, execute vt[0](in,out,0,0),
  read back byte flag + 16-byte value + two strings; one string
  is stored to a type-4 repository file named by content ID.
- FUN_6160571e ("ATPCD::BIND_CONTENT"): request = [buffer(
  repository-file bytes), int(input file size), octet16(vt+8),
  octet(input-name string), int(1), module("atpcd.ocm")];
  execute; read byte + status int (0 ok); then a second stream
  phase + file write (details in paper). It also uses a
  procfile.dll object (vt+0x28/0x2c/0x74) NOT present in the
  harness - BIND may be only partially replayable.
- RUN 123 PLAN: faithful OP-A replay in the harness using the
  LIVE ctx vtable slots (dump live vtable first - no address
  assumptions), dump the serialized request bytes from the
  stream object, execute, read back all outputs.

### Run 124 - salwrap1 address in session 5's notes was WRONG

Starting state re-verified from files: Sessions 5-9 read in full;

### DESK: BIND_CONTENT (FUN_6160571e) read COMPLETE, incl. helpers
Params: (arg1 = ptr to 16-byte content ID, arg2 = wide-string
INPUT FILE PATH, arg3 = out-dword ptr). All three must be non-null.
Full call sequence:
 1. OmgMmap(input path); size>=10 else exc 0xe; id3 =
    Atp::getId3TagSize(addr); size >= id3+0x60 else exc 0xe.
    hdr = mmap+id3 (secure-header ptr, saved).
 2. factory = procfile(); sess = factory->vt+8(hdr + 0x20)
    [session object created FROM the input's secure header+0x20];
    sess saved. sess->vt+0x74(0xe) == 0 -> destroy sess, ret
    0x80040418.
 3. repoPath = omgResolveRepositoryFile(4, contentID); open via
    omgutil9()->vt+0xc(path, 0xC0000000, 0,0, 3, 0,0) OPEN_EXISTING
    read/write; GetFileSize; ReadFile whole repo file into a
    buffer object {data@-0x4cc, size}.
 4. sIn/sOut = stream ctor (salwrap1 vt+0x40, cap 0x200) x2.
    moddesc = FUN_61604a42("atpcd.ocm").
 5. s = sess->vt+0x28(&tmpstring); ret stored over arg2 slot ->
    a STRING-OBJECT POINTER (its +4/+0x14/+0x18 are read as
    buf/size/cap by FUN_61604d81 = write-octet-of-string).
 6. Stream build (each writer returns the stream handle):
    write-buffer-ref(sIn, {repo data ptr, repo size})   [+0x70]
    write-int(sIn, INPUT FILE SIZE)                     [+0x74]
    write-octet(sIn, {vt+8 ptr, 16})  (FUN_616048d7)    [+0x6c]
    write-octet(sIn, string-from-step-5) (FUN_61604d81) [+0x6c]
    write-int(sIn, 1)            <- selector 1          [+0x74]
    write-module(sIn, moddesc)   (appends 01 00 0b)     [+0x64]
    [CORRECTION to Session 9's summary: phase 1 has SIX fields;
    the 4th is the procfile-supplied string, not the input-name
    string - the input NAME never enters the stream at all.]
 7. execute vt[0](sIn, sOut, 0, 0).
 8. read-byte(sOut) -> flag; read-int(sOut) -> STATUS (-0x478).
    log "ATPCD::BIND_CONTENT: 0x" + hex(contentID16).
    status > 99 -> procfile()->vt+0x58(status-100, log, 0, 0).
 9. status == 1/2/3/other -> named exceptions (INVALID_DATA /
    DATA_SIZE_OVER / FILE_NO_OVER / unknown). NO success path.
10. status == 0 ("phase 2", ALL LOCAL DRIVER BOOKKEEPING):
    a. FUN_6160547d() prepares two string destinations (split
       helpers over the manager/repo objects);
       read-string(sOut) x2 -> strA(-0x70), strB(@*-0x4dc);
       read-string(sOut) -> strC(-0x30); read-string -> strD
       (@*-0x4dc + 0x1c); read-int(sOut) -> N; read-buf(sOut,
       desc{ptr=local8, len=8}) -> SEED8.
       *arg3 = N.
    b. salwrap0()->vt+0xc(&objE0) [fills a 16-B range/vector
       object]; FUN_616053af(&objE0) frees it after (d).
    c. sess->vt+0x2c(strA, 1).
    d. SetFilePointer(repoHandle, 0, FILE_BEGIN)==0 ->
       WriteFile(repoHandle, strC.data, strC.size) [repository
       file OVERWRITTEN from offset 0 with a SAL-returned string];
       SecureAudioHeader::setEkbVersion(hdr+0x10, 0x1000E);
       memcpy(hdr+0x18, SEED8, 8)  [hdr+0x18 == BlockSeed field];
       FUN_616053af(&objE0); cleanup; return 0.
VERDICT: **BIND_CONTENT contains NO bulk transform and NO second
SAL request.** The input payload is never read (mmap used only
for id3 size + header ptr), never written except the 8-byte
BlockSeed + EKB version in the header. The SAL op (selector 1)
is a key-management/binding op: it consumes the repository blob
+ the procfile-supplied string and returns binding strings + a
fresh BlockSeed. The audio transform is NOT in omgatpcd's
BIND_CONTENT at all -> CASE (b) territory: the transform must
live in procfile.dll (session object or another entry) or in a
component not yet examined. Next: locate procfile.dll, map the
session object's FULL vtable, find a data-processing method.

### DESK before run 129 — the comparison is INSIDE maclist.ocm
- The exception TEXT lives in salwrap.dll, not pfcom: "bad
  ProcessFile MAC" @ VA 0x651396a0, xref 0x6510400f, inside
  **FUN_65103ee0** (decomp-key.c:441) = salwrap's ICV status-code
  translator: switch(code) {1:"bad token"(0x303), 2:"bad
  ProcessFile MAC"(0x303), 3:"OpenMG oh my god!!!"(0x302),
  4:"bad newest ekb version", 5:"bad ekb format", 7:exc 0x18,
  <100:"icv.ocm: unknown return status", >=100: FUN_6510dd30
  (code-100)}. So "MAC bad" = STATUS CODE 2 RETURNED BY A SAL
  OP, translated by salwrap. The MAC comparison itself happens
  INSIDE the maclist.ocm VM program, not in pfcom native code.
- The caller pattern (FUN_65104eb0, decomp-key.c:974): a salwrap
  helper builds module desc "maclist.ocm", two streams, writes
  [vt+8 octet, buffer, buffer, int, module], executes vt[0],
  reads byte (+0x8c) + int (+0x9c), then calls FUN_65103ee0
  (status,0); on success reads string (+0x80) + buffer (+0x84).
  A second helper FUN_65105170 (contains translator call site
  0x651050c1) has the same shape. pfcom imports ONLY
  salwrap0/1/2 from salwrap.dll -> pfcom reaches these helpers
  through salwrap0() vtable slots; the throw originates in
  salwrap, inside pfcom's vt+8 call stack.
- Run-128 pfcom-phase invokes, parsed exactly from run128.txt:
  INV1 (0x32 B) = [ref(20, keyblock@0x107e9240), ref(100,
  buf@0x107eb0b0), octet16(vt8), int(1), ref(maclist img),
  i1(0), END] -> c840 EAX=0x103e7738. (Session-10 note said the
  2nd ref was 40 B - WRONG for the pfcom phase; it is 100 B.
  The 40 B variant occurs in the b650-baseline ICV path.)
  INV2 (0x2d B) = [octet20(.opf key#1 d9ce...), ref(100, SAME
  buf), int(4), ref(maclist img), i1(0), END] -> EAX=0x103ec6c0.
  Then the throw. The 100-B buffer is shared by both invokes -
  the prime place for VM-written output.
- .opf full parse (232 B, BER/TLV, indefinite-length SEQ):
  INT 1002; OCT20 #1 @8 d9ce de ed...3e 20 54; OCT20 #2 @29
  da ed f9 1e...b2 19 75; SEQ{ INT 0x6b, INT 0x6aadeeaf,
  INT 7, INT 0x6ab04e20, INT 0x6d, INT 0, INT 0x65, OCT20 #3

  01 0f 50 00 00 04 00 00 00 4f 7e...f3 7f bb, INT 0x68,
  INT 0x3aa43d, INT 6, OCT(21) "raw content importing",
  INT 0x67, 2, 1, 3, 8, 3, 5, 4 }; SEQ{ OCT "_ESI", OCT20

#### SESSION 11 CONCLUSION — divergence localized to one named input

- The MAC comparison site (desk, verified live): there is NO comparison in
  pfcom. salwrap helper FUN_65105170 builds stream [oct20(.opf key #1 d9ce),
  ref100(MACLIST record), int 4, module maclist.ocm, int 0], executes it,
  reads byte + int status via manager readers +0x8c/+0x9c, and calls
  FUN_65103ee0(status): status 2 -> OMGException (0x303) "bad ProcessFile
  MAC". The entire verify is computed INSIDE maclist.ocm over those inputs.
- Byte-comparisons: the .opf's stored values (d9ce, daed, 33f9, _ESI 0a25,
  keyblock) occur NOWHERE in maclist.dat and are never output by any invoke;
  the invokes' refs are unchanged after execution; op 4's reply is just the
  status int. pfcom's own .opf TLV parsing is faithful (object dump).
- Divergence = INPUT SIDE, one input: the 100-byte MACLIST record's computed
  fields (16B at +0x40-ish, 8B tail) are keyed by the install's
  **IntegrityCheckID** registry GUID (software\sony corporation\openmg\
  IntegrityCheckID). Every file-side input is now a genuine corpus artifact
  (device.sal, omg_id.dat, maclist.dat/maclist1.dat, icv.dat staged run 131;
  harness-synthesized identity files backed up in scratch/identity-bak-129/).
  The GUID is generated once at first run on a real install and persists in
  its registry; the harness generates a fresh random one per run, so the
  blob it builds can never validate the genuine .opf (minted under the real
  GUID). Seeds prove tracking; only the REAL value can pass, and it is NOT
  in the corpus (searched: files, logs — omg_content.log is 1 byte; only
  omginit.exe code mentions the value name).
- FAITHFUL RECOVERY PATH (next session, needs Lola / the source machine):
  read IntegrityCheckID from the registry of the Windows install that
  produced the batch2 corpus (HKLM\SOFTWARE\WOW6432Node\Sony Corporation\
  OpenMG or the non-WOW node / HKCU equivalent — the harness reads the key
  'software\sony corporation\openmg' under HKLM in its mapping), seed it in
  REG_TREE exactly as runs 133/134 did, rerun: op 4 should return status 0,
  FUN_65105170 then reads back a string + two buffers (the unwrapped key
  material), the ProcessFile ctor completes, and factory vt+8 constructs
  the session. NO forgery was attempted or is needed — the check itself
  told us exactly which genuine value it wants.
- Harness state left behind: sal_emu129.py..sal_emu134.py + run129..134.txt;
  scratch now carries corpus identity files (device.sal/omg_id/maclist.dat/
  maclist1.dat/icv.dat staged); the GUID seed lines exist ONLY in

  GUID into a fresh copy of sal_emu132.py). StringFromGUID2 HLE added in
  sal_emu132.py (keep). Session object: NOT yet constructed — blocked

  run139's TRACE dump (trace128.txt, hardcoded legacy name) found

  the guest buffer via e.wr (host write — invisible to MEM_WRITE
  hooks, and h_ReadFile logs nothing). Just before it (TRACE
  9383): CreateFileW(path built from string @0x107e88e0 =

  and parses it into fields via memcpy stubs.

  phase): WriteFile(handle 0x144, src=0x107ea5a0, n=0x64) preceded
  by GetFileAttributesA(maclist.dat path @0x107e88e0),
  DeleteFileA(maclist1.dat path @0x107e8b00), CreateFileW(
  CREATE_ALWAYS). So a PRE-PROBE phase builds the record in
  buffer 0x107ea5a0 (call it REC2 — a second full record copy,
  confirmed in the end-of-run heap dump alongside the arena

  reads a persisted record; the building happens in whatever
  phase emitted TRACE #7036.**

  host_path() — the harness's REPO_WIN strings carry DOUBLE
  backslashes; the matched remainder begins with a separator, so
  os.path.join(SONY_DIR, '/maclist1.dat') discards SONY_DIR ->
  '/maclist1.dat'. Verified by direct host_path() calls. Corpus
  is never touched (corpus maclist.dat/maclist1.dat are the
  21,420-B data files, untouched, Oct 3 mtimes).

  session-create performed the ENTIRE ICV sequence itself, in
  order (all file143-logged): read .opf (232 B) -> open
  system32\icv.dat -> ReadFile <REPO>\maclist.dat (100 B, the
  persisted stable record) -> read OMGKEY\omg_id.dat -> fetch
  maclist.ocm -> read <REPO>\maclist2.dat (40-B form) -> write
  <REPO>\maclist1.dat (40 B) -> CREATE an OMGRIGHT\<sha256-hex>
  .ICV rights file -> read maclist1.dat (40 B) -> fetch
  init2.ocm -> WriteFile <REPO>\maclist2.dat (100 B record) ->
  create a second OMGRIGHT .ICV -> ReadFile maclist2.dat (100 B)
  -> PF op 1 (passes) -> PF op 4 (status 2). The sequence's own
  invokes are mac #1-#4 (int5 40-B, int5 100-B, op 0, op 1);
  #5/#6 are the PF op 1/op 4.
- => **The ICV refresh is a ONCE-PER-PROCESS engine operation**:
  whichever comes first — the harness's baseline control or
  session-create — performs it; the other skips it. (Run 140's
  WriteFile sat in controls-baseline because the baseline got
  there first; Session 12's "ctor builds the record in-window"
  was this same refresh seen from the other side.) GENERAL
  MODEL: op 1 (re)builds+persists the record; the ctor loads it
  via ReadFile; op 4 verifies [.opf key#1 x record].
- **The 21,420-B maclist DATABASE is never read in this flow**
  (fetches are .ocm only; DB-consult hypothesis dead). Op 4's
  whole world: .opf key #1, the 100-B record, GUID-derived
  state, the maclist VM.

  6972a715..., omg_id.dat 4f71de38..., device.sal 68c4f09c...,
  .opf 21a07d83...). Corpus untouched.

### Session 13 summary (runs 138-143) — localization delivered

- **Writer-PC log for the sealed fields (step 2's aim)**: there
  are NO guest writes that compute the seal. Run 139's watch
  caught only a 100-B memset (OMGUtils pc=0x639029e0). The seal
  is computed INSIDE maclist.ocm's VM during op 1; custody:
  VM result arena --memcpy pc=0x65106e69--> stream buffer
  --memcpy pc=0x65106e69--> REC2 buffer --WriteFile--> rotation
  file (/maclist{,1,2}.dat at the VM root; the harness's
  host_path quirk lands them at host '/') --ReadFile (ctor)-->
  op-4 ref100. The ctor does NOT build the record (corrects
  Session 12's model; its "two generations" = the rotation).
- **Time (candidate a): DEMOTED/FALSIFIED at tested
  granularity.** Census (run 140): the only nondeterministic
  harness inputs are wall-clock time and the persisted lineage;
  rand is a deterministic LCG (srand never called), QPC/
  GetTickCount/clock are deterministic counters (candidate c
  falsified with it). Frozen-time runs 141/142 and real-time
  run 140 produced a BYTE-IDENTICAL record. The 135->140
  variation was lineage convergence (each run's refresh fed on
  the previous run's record: 7a06->a323->11b7->5705->ba05->
  8cf0->476d), reaching a fixed point at run 140 that has held
  for three runs (140/141/142) and through run 143's in-ctor
  refresh.
- **VM history (candidate b): FALSIFIED in harness-controllable
  form.** Minimal flow (143): a fresh post-bootstrap VM, the
  engine's own canonical ICV sequence inside session-create,
  the stable record, frozen time — build and verify in the same
  process still disagree; op 4 = status 2. Standard flow
  (141/142, baselines first) also status 2.
- **Op 4 status in EVERY configuration tried (135-143):
  status 2.** No session object in any run. No readback to
  capture (step 5's success branch never triggered).
- **SURVIVING CANDIDATE**: the verify pairs the record against
  the .opf, minted on the Toshiba against the TOSHIBA's record
  lineage. The harness lineage descends from record files the
  harness itself created (in the synthetic-identity era,
  pre-Session-11) and has converged to A fixed point of the
  refresh map — unproven to be THE Toshiba install's record.
  If the map has multiple fixed points (or carries lineage
  state beyond the sealed fields), op 4 cannot pass in this
  lineage with this .opf.
- **NEXT PRECISE EXPERIMENTS**: (i) FRESH-LINEAGE ARM — move
  the '/'-root rotation files aside (harness state, not
  corpus), let the engine create the record from nothing under
  the genuine GUID + genuine identity files, iterate the
  standard flow to convergence, test op 4: same fixed point
  (476d...) => uniqueness, pairing problem moves to the
  .opf/EKB side; different fixed point => lineage-dependence
  proven and the Toshiba's own current record state becomes
  the missing input. (ii) OP-4 REPLY DIFF — dump invoke #6's
  full reply stream (the helper reads byte+status only; the
  stream may carry more) and diff any recomputed fields against

### Session 15 summary (runs 147-149) — the path audit found and fixed TWO real misroutes; op 4 still fails with a provably clean access picture

- **Misroute #1 (prefix/join discard) — FOUND (147),
  FIXED (148).** host_path()'s os.path.join discarded the
  mapped host base whenever the guest's post-prefix
  remainder began with a separator — i.e. for EVERY
  Repository-relative path the guest builds. Through run
  147 no guest access ever reached the repository folder:
  'REPO\maclist.dat' read a 100-B standalone file at host
  '/'; the .opf was read from a staged system32 copy after
  the repository procfile/ probe FAILED; the 21,420-B
  maclist database was never actually read (the guest
  sizes its read by GetFileSize, so the 'DB read'
  silently degenerated to n=100). Fix: strip leading
  separators from the remainder before joining (Windows
  semantics) + point the REPO mapping at a byte-identical
  mirror of the corpus folder (guest writes behave as on
  the real folder; corpus evidence never modified).
  Under the fix (148): zero system32 fallbacks; both DBs
  read in full (n=21420) for the first time ever; .opf
  read from its real repository path; procfile probe
  succeeds first-try.
- **Misroute #2 (case sensitivity) — FOUND (148),
  FIXED (149).** The guest probes for 'EKB'; the folder
  on disk is 'ekb' (lowercase, in both the batch2 corpus
  and Lola's live Toshiba folder). NTFS would match; the
  harness's case-sensitive host FS did not, even after
  fix #1. Fix: case-insensitive component resolution in
  host_path (only converts currently-failing lookups).
  Under the fix (149): EKB probes succeed; the fallback
  probe disappears. (No file under ekb/ is opened in
  this flow — presence check only, no downstream change.)
- **Op 4 outcome: status 2 in 147, 148 AND 149.** The
  success path never triggered; no readback, no session
  object, no querycif/GET_GLOBAL_SEED exposure, no
  ciphertext. The fixes were necessary for harness
  fidelity (the harness now presents the Toshiba's real
  filesystem semantics: correct prefix resolution,
  correct case behavior, genuine files at genuine paths)
  but they are NOT the cause of the MAC failure. Every
  input to the verify is now provably genuine: .opf key
  #1 from the genuine .opf read in-window at its real
  path; the 476d record (deterministic in genuine
  constants, Session 14); the genuine maclist.ocm image
  (d3c866f7, staged==corpus); the genuine IntegrityCheckID
  (Session 12); both maclist databases physically
  present at the real path and fully read.
- **NEXT (named per the audit, .opf/EKB side): trace the
  provenance of the 16-byte EKB id (the octet16) that
  op 1's stream carries and that op 4's recomputation
  presumably keys on.** In the harness it enters as a
  manager/vt+8 value; NO .STK or .ekb file content is
  ever loaded in any run (the audit shows zero accesses
  to ekb/* files or any STK name — the EKB dir is only
  presence-probed). On the real install, EKB material
  for version 0x1000E (the .opf's EkbVersion; BIND writes
  hdr+0x10 = 0x1000E) ships as 0001000E.STK (172 B, byte-
  identical on both of Lola's reference discs; a loader
  for it already exists in the atrac-cd-author publish

## Session 16 — runs 150+ (2026-10-29): EKB-id provenance — the "EKB id" is the OMG ID; hypothesis reframed by the decompilation

Desk results BEFORE any run (verified from papers + run149.txt + corpus bytes):

- **The octet16 in every native maclist invoke of run 149 (#1,#2,#3,#4,#5) is
  byte-identical:** `01 0f 50 00 00 04 00 00 00 00 00 16 72 5f 9b e7`. The
  harness's own vt8-getter probe returns the same 16 bytes (ptr 0x107e6ef4).
  Op 4 (invoke #6) carries NO octet16 — its stream is [oct20 key#1, ref100
  record, int 4, module, int 0] only, so op 4 keys on VM/native state set by
  the earlier invokes.
- **Manager vt+8 = FUN_6511a4a0 (decomp-key.c:7930).** It calls
  FUN_65119b20(&DAT_6516b0ac) (lazy init, returns &string at holder+0x20);
  if the string's size == 0 it THROWS OMGException 0x12 naming
  "OMGKEY/omg_sal.dat"; otherwise it returns string_data + 4 in BOTH the
  heap branch (*(str+4)+4) and the SSO branch (str+8 = buf+4). Callers read
  16 bytes there. **vt+8 = "give me bytes [4:20] of the identity string".**
- **The loader FUN_65119b20 (decomp-key.c:7607)** populates that string once
  (flag at holder+0x34): (a) if omgResolveRepositoryFile("OMGKEY/omg_id.dat")
  succeeds -> OmgString::readFromFile loads the file into the string;
  (b) else if "OMGKEY/omg_sal.dat" resolves -> take its first 0x16 bytes,
  zero bytes [20] and [21], and WRITE the result as omg_id.dat;
  (c) else -> GENERATE via an icv.ocm SAL invoke (manager vt+0x40/+0x74/
  +0x64/execute/+0x80 read-string), write omg_id.dat, and omglog
  "omgid generated: %02X..." over data[4:20].
- **The corpus omg_id.dat (22 B, MD5 4f71de38...) is:**
  `30 80 04 10 | 01 0f 50 00 00 04 00 00 00 00 00 16 72 5f 9b e7 | 00 00`.
  Bytes [4:20] == the observed octet16 EXACTLY. The [20:22]=00 00 matches
  branch (b)'s zeroing — the Toshiba's omg_id.dat was itself derived from an
  omg_sal.dat at install time. The 5-byte per-install unique part is
  [15:20]=`16 72 5f 9b e7` (the Session-11 "genuine tail"); the harness's old
  synthetic (identity-bak-129) shares the 11-byte constant prefix but has
  tail `76 3c 65 6f f5`.
- **Run 149's loader took branch (a) on the genuine file:** [hle] _access +
  CreateFile on mirror OMGKEY/omg_id.dat (exists=True) at run149.txt:7753/
  7758, before invoke #1 (line 7969); no "omgid generated" in run 149.
  Octet16 is byte-identical in runs 147, 148 AND 149 (147's loader also
  reached a genuine copy). **CONCLUSION: the 16-byte value is the OMG ID
  core — NOT an EKB id, NOT .STK-derived. The "EKB id" label (Session 10's
  shorthand, propagated by later briefs) is a misnomer. The value the
  harness feeds the verify is already the genuine install value, correctly
  loaded from the genuine file at its genuine path.**
- **EKB landscape (goal 3 inventory):** salwrap.dll exports ONLY salwrap0/
  1/2. Internal EKB machinery exists but is a SEPARATE subsystem:
  FUN_65105f40 = an ekbman.ocm SAL driver (by-name lookup FUN_65103d10/
  FUN_65103c90, special-cases "msa"; status!=0,3 -> throw "EKB: %s: %d");
  FUN_651065f0 = "newest EKB" loader (NEWEKB_TXT resource via
  omgGetResourceData, else ekb/newekb.txt, FindFirst EKB/newekb*.txt;
  errors "bad newest ekb version"/"bad ekb format" are status texts in
  FUN_65103ee0's table); FUN_65109c90 = EKB/ *.ekb enumeration + ekb/kdrm;
  FUN_6510d560 = GetLocalEKB (parses .ekb tag data; REVOKED_ID checks).
  xrefs.txt: FUN_651065f0 and FUN_65109c90 have exactly 1 COMPUTED_CALL
  xref each (dispatcher-table driven). Corpus OpenMG top level HAS
  ekbman.ocm; live ekb/ holds 00000002.ekb, 00010001.ekb, 00010002.ekb,
  0001000A.ekb, 00010012-umd.EKB, 00010013-hdd.EKB, 00010017-hdd.EKB,
  0001001D-bot.EKB, 00010021-hdd.EKB, kdrm/ — NO 0001000E anything.
  0001000E.STK (172 B) exists only on the reference DISC roots
  (atrac-cd-stage0/CD stuff/ and disc2/); it is a disc-side artifact for
  the authoring app, not an install-folder input to this verify path.
  omgatpcd's setEkbVersion(0x1000e) is a hard-coded constant.
  pfcom.dll imports salwrap0/1/2 (so it CAN reach the dispatcher).
  **In runs 147/148/149: ekbman.ocm is NEVER fetched (grep count 0),
  no ekb/* file is ever opened, no newekb access — the only EKB contact
  is the two _access dir probes (now passing). Whether ANY of the four

- Remaining plan: run 150 = sal_emu149 lineage + pristine mirror +

### Session 16 summary (run 150) — the EKB-id hypothesis is DEAD; the value is the OMG ID, already genuine

- **Provenance chain, complete:** manager vt+8 = FUN_6511a4a0 returns
  (identity-string data + 4); the string is loaded ONCE by
  FUN_65119b20 from repository file "OMGKEY/omg_id.dat" via
  omgResolveRepositoryFile + OmgString::readFromFile (fallbacks:
  derive from omg_sal.dat, else generate via icv.ocm and log
  "omgid generated"). The 16 bytes in every maclist invoke are
  omg_id.dat[4:20] — the OMG ID core: DER-ish header `30 80 04 10`,
  11 constant bytes, the 5-byte per-install unique tail
  `16 72 5f 9b e7`, `00 00`. Live-proven in run 150: the in-memory
  string is byte-identical to the genuine corpus file.
- **The .STK/EKB theory is disproved, not merely unsupported:** the
  EKB subsystem (ekbman.ocm driver, newest-EKB loader, .ekb
  enumeration, GetLocalEKB) is a separate device-EKB mechanism that
  this flow NEVER enters (x0 entries across the whole run incl.
  bootstrap); no .STK or ekb/* content is loaded by any code path
  feeding the verify; 0x1000E enters omgatpcd only as a hard-coded
  EkbVersion constant. The staging experiment was therefore not run
  — there is no load point for an STK in this path, and staging one
  would be theater, not fidelity. Session 10's "EKB id" naming was
  the mislabel; every later brief inherited it.
- **Op 4 still returns status 2** with: genuine GUID (seeded),
  genuine record (deterministic fixed point), genuine databases
  read in full at genuine paths, genuine .opf at its genuine path,
  and now a live-proven genuine OMG ID. Every NATIVE-side input to
  the verify is accounted for and genuine.
- **NEXT (named, .opf/VM side): a VM-service-callback audit of the
  op-4 execution.** Session 15 audited the NATIVE file/registry
  layer; what has never been enumerated is what the maclist VM
  itself requests from the host DURING op 4's execution (service

## Session 17 — runs 151+ (2026-10-29): VM-service-callback audit — op 1 vs op 4 host-service diff

Brief: enumerate everything the maclist VM's execution causes the host
(harness) to answer during PF op 4 vs PF op 1; hunt for a stub/default/
fabricated answer where the real install would answer install-specific.

### Desk map BEFORE any run (verified from sal_emu150.py source + papers + PE bytes)

- **Architecture of the surface.** salwrap.dll + OMGUtils.dll run as REAL
  native code; the SAL interpreter (FUN_6512c840 "vminvoke") and all SAL
  "services" are native salwrap functions — faithful by construction.
  The interpreter's registered error callback is FUN_65116480
  ("SAL INTERNAL ERROR" raiser, registered via FUN_65125350 in
  FUN_6511C8A0 — decomp-key.c:7315/8807). The ONLY layer the harness
  answers is the Win32/CRT import surface: every import of the loaded
  PEs is routed to a stub page; Emu.on_stub dispatches to the HLE dict;
  an import with NO HLE entry halts the run as UNIMPLEMENTED (none has
  fired in runs 105-150). GetProcAddress for an unimplemented name
  returns a working stub whose handler is h_noop0 (answers 0).
  No Crypt*/advapi32 imports exist in salwrap/OMGUtils/pfcom/omgatpcd
  (pefile-verified) — SAL crypto is native code, no crypto HLE exists.
- **FLAGGED FABRICATION #1 — CoCreateGuid.** BATCH2 registers
  ole32!CoCreateGuid as `(lambda e,a,x: (e.wr(a[0], bytes(range(16))),0)[1])`
  — it writes the CONSTANT bytes 00 01 02 ... 0f and returns S_OK.
  (The Session-11 brief's "deterministic counter" description was
  another garble: it is a constant, not a counter.) Both salwrap.dll
  (IAT 0x65139338) and OMGUtils.dll (IAT 0x639223e0) import it.
  Capstone sweep: OMGUtils has exactly ONE direct call site
  (0x639031c2); salwrap has no direct call to its slot (may be unused
  or reached indirectly). **The Session-16 "unidentified ref16" is
  explained:** salwrap .data @RVA 0x44f44 (VA 0x65144f44) is ALL ZEROS
  in the file image (pefile-verified); the live content 00 01..0f can
  only have been written by this lambda into a static GUID buffer,
  whose bytes are then passed by ref into maclist invokes #1/#2.
  Live provenance (which call, which target) is run 151's census job.
- **FLAGGED cluster #2 — SID/token answers (all fabricated-plausible).**
  GetTokenInformation: TokenUser = fixed SID RID 1000; TokenGroups =
  exactly two fixed groups (Administrators 544 + Users 545, attrs 7)
  (rewritten run 106 for the OMGUtils privilege classifier at
  0x6391a0d0, after run 94's EqualSid fault). OpenProcessToken returns
  a fake handle 0x600. AllocateAndInitializeSid/InitializeSid build
  structurally real SIDs from caller args (faithful mechanics).
  EqualSid does a REAL byte comparison of the two SIDs (h_EqualSid_log).
  Security-descriptor getters answer empty-DACL constants (h_ret1 etc.).
- **FLAGGED constants #3 — environment answers.** GetVersion ->
  0x0A280105 (XP); GetVersionExA -> 5.1 build 2600 SP2;
  SHGetFolderPathA -> hardcoded 'C:\Users\Glitched7\...' profile paths
  (the Toshiba user — real-install-derived constants);
  GetCommandLineA -> 'salhost.exe'; GetModuleFileNameA -> mapped fake
  paths; _getpid -> 4242; CreateThread -> thread NOT run (single-thread
  harness), fake handle, tid 999; CoCreateInstance -> 0x80040154
  (REGDB_E_CLASSNOTREG) always; FormatMessageA -> generic success text;
  GetLocaleInfoA -> 'ENU'/mostly empty; FindResource/LoadResource/
  LockResource/SizeofResource -> h_noop0 (0); GetDiskFreeSpace* ->
  fixed 8 GiB free; GlobalMemoryStatus* -> fixed 2 GiB/50%;
  GetProcessAffinityMask -> mask 1.
- **Time/RNG (already falsified in Session 13, recorded for the census):**
  h_time frozen at 1793313847 (line 578) + GetLocalTime/
  GetSystemTimeAsFileTime aligned to the same instant (harness
  condition); GetTickCount/QPC deterministic counters (+37/+100);
  rand = deterministic MSVC LCG, srand never called by guest in any
  observed run.
- **h_noop0 (answers 0) covers 37 names** (critical sections, atexit,
  FreeSid, Suspend/ResumeThread, FindResource family...) and **h_ret1
  (answers 1) covers 33** (SEH frame handlers, FreeLibrary, HeapValidate,
  most security-descriptor setters...). Full key->handler census was
  extracted mechanically from sal_emu150.py during prep.
- **Faithful/computed (not flagged):** file layer (real mirror files,
  Session 15), registry layer (REG_TREE incl. genuine GUID seed),
  StringFromGUID2/UuidFromString (real conversions), CRT string/memory
  (real), std::string HLE (real state), allocator (real), strtok
  (stateful, real), GetFileSize/ReadFile/WriteFile (real).
- **Interpreter dispatch check:** bytecode-level "host calls" resolve
  to native salwrap service functions; the divergence surface is the
  HLE layer above. No evidence in the papers of bytecode calling
  function pointers supplied by the embedding exe (in the real process
  Omgjbox hosts salwrap the same way the harness script does: D680
  ctor + C8A0 init perform the registrations natively).

### Run 151 results (VERIFIED by reading run151.txt)

STARTING LINE REPRODUCED: ctx 0x100002e0; getimage == device.sal at all
three checkpoints; PF op 1 passes; FUN_65103ee0 status=2 at ret
0x65105355 (the op-4 throw). Invoke map (this run's numbering):
#1/#2 = 61-B DB invokes [int0, ref16, octet16(OMG ID), ref21420(DB),
ref20, int5, module, int0] -> status 0; #3 = 32-B [octet16, int0,
module, int0]; #4 = 50-B op 1 (baseline lineage: ref40 prev record);
#5 = 50-B op 1 (PF window, ref100 = the 476d fixed-point record);
#6 = 45-B OP 4 inside FUN_65105170 [oct20(key#1), ref100(SAME buffer
0x107f8150 as #5), int4, module, int0] -> status 2.

*** LIVE DIFF — THE INVOKE WINDOWS ARE SERVICE-CLEAN. Every maclist
invoke window (#1..#6) contains EXACTLY the same 10 distinct HLE
services: VirtualProtect, GetThreadContext, SetThreadContext,
SetThreadAffinityMask, GetCurrentProcess, GetCurrentThread,
GetProcessAffinityMask, GetCurrentThreadId, EnterCriticalSection,
LeaveCriticalSection — all interpreter machinery (the FUN_6512c840
prologue's Dr-mailbox + affinity dance and the engine's code-patching).
NO file, registry, token, GUID, time, rand, COM or GetProcAddress call
fires inside ANY invoke window. Op 4's window (#6, 303 calls) differs
from op 1's (#5, 347) only in count, not in kind. The VM requests
NOTHING install-specific from the host while executing maclist
bytecode; every verify input arrives via the stream. The callback
hypothesis in its narrow form (a stub fired DURING op 4) is DEAD.

*** BUT THE CENSUS FOUND THE REAL DIVERGENCE — ONE WINDOW EARLIER.
ole32!CoCreateGuid fires EXACTLY ONCE in the whole run, in the boot
window, BEFORE invoke #1: args[0] = 0x65144f44 (proved: the Session-16
ref16 buffer IS salwrap's static GUID buffer in .data), caller
retaddr 0x6510783f (salwrap). The surrounding sequence, live-logged:
  RegQueryValueExA(hkey 0x824, value @0x6513937c "TypeLib") -> 0x2
  (NOT FOUND) retaddr 0x651077ce
  CoCreateGuid(&DAT_65144f44) -> harness writes CONSTANT 00 01..0f
  StringFromGUID2 -> "{03020100-0504-0706-0809-0A0B0C0D0E0F}"
  RegSetValueExA -> [reg set] software\classes\clsid\{69c1dd9e-87e7-
  40f3-877e-da23063e8eb8}\dependency\TypeLib = that fake string
Aligned disassembly at 0x65107807..0x65107873 (in FUN_651069f0; error
tags "gBIS-2/-3/-4" @0x6513987c/874/86c): IIDFromString (IAT
0x65139348) parses a stored string into the buffer when the query
hits; CoCreateGuid (IAT 0x65139338) + StringFromGUID2 (IAT
0x65139330) + registry persist is the generate-once fallback. So on a
real install this GUID is generated ONCE, persisted in the registry,
and read back every process thereafter; the 16 bytes are then passed
by ref into maclist invokes #1/#2 (the DB invokes) — the ONLY
stream input in the whole session-create flow whose harness value is
fabricated. (The OMGUtils CoCreateGuid call site @0x639031c2 never
fired; the boot SID/token cluster fired only in boot, from OMGUtils'
privilege classifier — not on any invoke path.)

*** THE GENUINE VALUE — FOUND IN LOLA'S OWN TOSHIBA .reg EXPORT
(read-only; UTF-16LE search). The CLSID is Omgconv2.OmgEat3ToPcm's.
The real install (32-bit process -> Wow6432Node redirection) serves:
  HKLM\SOFTWARE\Classes\Wow6432Node\CLSID\{69C1DD9E-87E7-40F3-877E-
  DA23063E8EB8}\Dependency  "TypeLib" = "{317B92BE-B04A-4EF6-BC04-
  02346DEFDB20}"
(identical entry also at HKLM\SOFTWARE\Wow6432Node\Classes\CLSID\
{69C1DD9E...}\Dependency). Expected buffer bytes after IIDFromString:
be 92 7b 31 4a b0 f6 4e bc 04 02 34 6d ef db 20.
FAITHFUL-FIX PLAN (run 152): seed REG_TREE with the value the real
install serves for the key the guest opens (harness path spelling
software\classes\clsid\{69c1dd9e...}\dependency, value "TypeLib",
data "{317B92BE-B04A-4EF6-BC04-02346DEFDB20}") — the IntegrityCheckID
seed pattern, genuine data only. Then: the query must HIT,
IIDFromString must parse the genuine bytes into 0x65144f44,
CoCreateGuid must NOT fire, invokes #1/#2 must carry the genuine
ref16 — and op 4 gets its verdict with the last fabricated input in
the flow removed. (Whether maclist's op 4 actually depends on this
GUID via VM state persisted by #1/#2 is exactly what the run tests;
#1/#2 pass either way, so a status change can only come from op 4's
own check.)

### Run 152 setup
sal_emu152.py = sal_emu151.py (census retained, relabeled) + TWO
faithful changes: (1) REG_TREE seed of the genuine gBIS TypeLib GUID
"{317B92BE-B04A-4EF6-BC04-02346DEFDB20}" under the dependency key the

### Run 152 — TWO executions, one filename (process caveat + results)

The run152 compound command executed TWICE under the same session id
(the runtime replayed it; the second execution truncated and
overwrote run152.txt — the first execution's log survives only as
grep excerpts captured in-session). The two runs differ ONLY in
starting mirror state, which turns out to matter:

RUN 152-A (pristine mirror + genuine gBIS GUID) — the controlled
experiment. Captured from its own completion grep + immediate reads:
seed logged; ctx 0x100002e0; IIDFromString parsed the genuine GUID
(be 92 7b 31 4a b0 f6 4e bc 04 02 34 6d ef db 20) into 0x65144f44;
CoCreateGuid NEVER fired (0 mentions); invokes #1/#2 carried the
genuine ref16; flow identical to run 151 (6 invokes); FUN_65103ee0
status=2 at ret 0x65105355 — **OP 4 STILL FAILS with the genuine GUID
in place. The gBIS-GUID hypothesis is FALSIFIED as the cause** (the
divergence was real and is now removed; it was not the blocker).

RUN 152-B (log preserved as run152b-evolved-mirror.txt; mirror
inherited run A's guest-evolved state): invoke #2 — the identical
61-B DB invoke — ran 17,450 HLE calls incl. 8,570 malloc/free pairs
(vs ~300 calls on pristine state): maclist's DB op is strongly
state-sensitive to the repository DB contents the previous run left
behind. The session-create then died EARLIER and DIFFERENTLY: the
ProcessFile ctor was entered but factory vt+8 stopped on an
unhandled CxxThrowException whose harness handler errored ("guest
called abort/exit"); NO (0x303), NO op-1/op-4 invokes inside the
ctor. B is contaminated evidence (non-pristine start) — recorded as
a state-sensitivity lead only, not a verdict.

Run 153 re-runs the controlled experiment (pristine mirror,
sal_emu152.py unchanged, fresh output filename) to obtain one
authoritative clean log.

### Run 153 results (authoritative; single execution, completion
verified by process exit + stable file size before analysis;
sal_emu152.py unchanged; mirror pristine, all five MD5s verified)

Run 153 REPRODUCES run 152-B marker-for-marker (ctx line 7239,
IIDFromString line 7707, invoke #2 RETURN line 35524, census lines
38103/38114) — so 152-B's divergence was NOT caused by its evolved
mirror. Revised interpretation of 152-A: its captured excerpts (op 4
status 2 under the genuine GUID) are NOT reproducible and its log is
lost; treat 152-A as an unresolved anomaly (the session's command
executed more than once under one id and the file was rewritten
between reads). The reproducible facts are 151 vs 152-B/153:

- Controls green in 153: getimage == device.sal at all three
  checkpoints; ctx 0x100002e0; CoCreateGuid 0 mentions; the genuine
  GUID (be 92 7b 31 4a b0 f6 4e bc 04 02 34 6d ef db 20) sits in
  0x65144f44 and is carried by invokes #1 AND #2.
- *** INVOKE #2 CHANGES COMPLETELY under the genuine GUID. Same
  61-byte DB invoke, but: 17,450 HLE calls (vs 304 in run 151) incl.
  8,570 malloc/free pairs, and the reply is a ~15.4 KB stream with
  status int 1 (vs status 0 and a stub reply under the fake GUID)
  embedding a full MACLIST image. The gBIS GUID is load-bearing for
  maclist's DB op: the fake constant made it short-circuit; the
  genuine value makes it do the full DB processing and return an
  updated/processed DB payload with status 1.
- *** THE SESSION-CREATE THEN STOPS EARLIER THAN OP 4, somewhere new.
  Only 2 maclist invokes occur in the whole run. Inside factory vt+8
  the ProcessFile ctor is entered, reads maclist1.dat in full
  (21,420 B, MD5 2d026c13) at its real mirror path, and the guest
  then constructs an OMGException — message pieces swprintf'd as
  "(0x2)", "<8858>", " - OpenMG oh my god!!" — and the harness's
  _CxxThrowException handling itself errors: "handler error in
  msvcr71.dll!_CxxThrowException: RuntimeError('guest called
  abort/exit')" (run153.txt line 37131). No (0x303), no PF op 1,
  no op 4 invoke. So: op 4's status under the genuine GUID is
  UNKNOWN — the blocker moved upstream. Two candidate layers, in
  order: (a) whatever the ctor does with invoke #2's status-1/15 KB
  result (and the exception it throws after reading maclist1.dat —
  identify the exception code/message properly), and (b) a harness
  SEH-fidelity gap: this throw path breaks the harness's exception
  processing where the (0x303) path did not.

- Harness SEH model (sal_emu152.py, read directly): `run_with_seh` only
  dispatches on Unicorn FAULTS (AV/invalid-insn/int3). `_CxxThrowException`
  is bound to `h_abort` (HLE table line 685) which raises a Python
  RuntimeError immediately — the guest's C++ catch machinery is NEVER
  invoked for ANY throw in this harness (the (0x303) path died the same
  way; it was simply the accepted end-state there). msvcr71's
  __CxxFrameHandler is an HLE stub address, and seh_dispatch only
  accepts handler addresses inside salwrap/OMGUtils ranges. So the
  "handler error" is STRUCTURAL: no C++ throw can dispatch in the
  current harness. Whether faithful dispatch changes the outcome
  depends entirely on whether guest code between the throw site and
  vt+8's return has a catch for this exception — desk (decomp) + the
  throw-site capture decide.

  maclist/ICV load-verify method of salwrap's DB-state object. It scans its three DB
  filename strings (this+0x30, stride 0x1c) for the first existing file, then rotates

  and FUN_65103e50 threw (0x2) before the verify step (FUN_65108840) was ever reached.**
  FUN_65109380 has 16 call sites, all inside the salwrap DB subsystem (0x651097xx-
  0x6510c7xx); in the vt+8 flow it is reached from pfcom's ProcessFile ctor via the
  node2 salwrap wrapper frame. WHY the object's +0 is null in the harness flow is the

  the probe sequence never performs) — but it is a GENUINE guest state assertion, not an
  emulation artifact: the code, the object, and the null are all really there.

  NO catch clause for OMGExceptionImpl (or anything) for the emulator to dispatch to.
  On a real machine this exception propagates out of factory vt+8 to the host app
  (Omgjbox level — the designed handling layer for OMGException, the modules' standard
  error channel). The harness's abort-at-_CxxThrowException end-state therefore
  FAITHFULLY represents "vt+8 raised OMGException(0x2)"; it is not a fidelity gap, and

### Desk facts established BEFORE run 156 (all verified from the binaries/decomp myself)

- **FUN_65103e50 re-read** (112 B, capstone): `ecx=*this; if 0 -> throw
  OMGException(2,"OpenMG oh my god!!",line 91); else tail-call
  OMGUtils!OmgMmap::addr() with ECX=*this`. So **+0 is an OmgMmap\*** and
  the guard returns the mapped address.
- **FUN_65109380 re-read in full** (674 B): rotation over the 3 std::string
  filenames at this+0x30 (stride 0x1c; _Mysize +0x10/_Myres +0x14 layout);
  for the first existing file: OmgString::readFromFile(out_str, name,...)
  (reads into the CALLER's string, not the object), then
  FUN_65103e50(this) -> seed addr, then FUN_65108840(this, out_str,
  seed_addr, 0x14, &flag) — the ONLY call site of FUN_65108840 in the DLL.
  Tail: all-fail -> (0x50a) "restore ICV2" (line 0x173) if the
  omgResolveRepositoryFile("icv.dat"@0x65139898, this+0x10) + FUN_65108ea0
  path passes, else (0x302) "OpenMG oh my god!" (line 0x175). Success ->
  optional FUN_65107d70 / FUN_651069f0 under this+0xb8.
- **The two wrappers** (both push the shared EH stub 0x65136618 — the
  Session-18 "wrapper frame"): **FUN_651099c0** = lock; FUN_65109380(this,
  &local); **FUN_65105170(this, &local, arg1, arg2)** (the op-4 helper);
  unlock; ret 8. **FUN_65109b90** = lock; if this+0x94==0:
  FUN_65109380(this,&local); FUN_65103e50(this);
  FUN_651053d0(this,&local,seed,this+0x80,this+0x9c); then string ops on
  +0x80/+0x9c; unlock; ret 8. Lock = the embedded object at this+4,
  vtable +8 lock / +0xc unlock.
- **Callers resolve the object through a lazy singleton getter.**
  Thunks: 0x6510ce50 (ret 4) -> FUN_6510cd50() -> FUN_65109810(obj);
  0x6510ce70 (ret 8) -> FUN_6510cd50() -> FUN_651099c0(obj,...);
  0x6510d0c0 (inside FUN_6510cf90-area fn) -> FUN_65109b90(obj,...).
  **FUN_6510cd50 (getter)**: lock guard object @0x65144f24; if global
  **[0x65144f20]==0**: obj = operator new(0x124); FUN_65104900(obj) ctor;
  store to [0x65144f20]; then **FUN_6510ca30(obj)** on EVERY getter call;
  unlock; return obj. pfcom imports ONLY salwrap0/1/2 from salwrap — all
  DB access funnels through these salwrap-side thunks.
- **FUN_65104900 (ctor, 399 B)**: +0 := 0 (NEVER set non-null in the ctor);
  +4 := OmgGlobalLock("OMG_ICV_LOCK"@0x65139744) — so this class IS the
  "ICV object"; +0x10 std::string; 3 filename std::strings at +0x30;
  OmgStrings at +0x80/+0x9c/+0x100 (inline layout: data +4, size +0x14,
  cap +0x18); +0xb8 byte := 1; +0xbc dword := 0; +0x11c := a 4-byte
  OmgMmap "OMG_KDRM_EVENT_COUNTER" (shared counter); +0x120 := its value.
  Object size 0x124.
- **FUN_6510ca30 (739 B) = the ensure-init method**, run by the getter on
  every call: if [this+0xbc]!=0 -> return immediately. Sets +0xbc=1;
  omgutil(2)!=0 -> throw (2,"Debugger is present",0xe7); locks; rebuilds
  the 3 filename strings as omgGetRepository()+table names
  (PTR table 0x651410cc: maclist2/1/.dat); resolves "icv.dat" into
  this+0x10 (bl); **FUN_65103dc0(this,&flagA)**; if flagA==0 -> done.
  If flagA!=0: seed=FUN_65103e50(this) (guard now passes);
  FUN_65116210(seed) valid -> done; else if bl and the FUN_65103a20
  (omgutil(4)!=0)+FUN_65114d50 gates pass: FUN_65108ea0(this,esi):
  fail -> restore block; pass + (esi==0 or *esi==0) -> **FUN_651048d0(this)
